# Consolidated reading: changes and decisions

Counts are recorded places, not changed tokens. One place may differ from both witnesses. Pure macro syntax is excluded. Retained doubts and certainty-only decisions are identified separately; they do not count as textual corrections unless the ink reading or its state changes. Where alternatives remain open, a counted disagreement concerns the primary printed reading and is not a claim that the competing reading has been ruled out. In the collation TSV, their_page follows the supplied convention: H page n corresponds to scan n+1.

## Consequential corrections

- Scan 036, main line 7: The capital alphabet is retained consistently.
- Scan 036, entire diagonal marginal NB, eight prose lines and formula with underbrace label: The note interprets the map using I-invariants; no claim about the missing earlier diagram (14) was verified.
- Scan 036, main line 18, whole disk formula and M0945: The written subscript 0 is preserved despite the surrounding discussion of a disk around a.
- Scan 038, inserted formula, arrow between the two pi expressions: Preserves the visibly directed isomorphism arrow.
- Scan 038, line beginning de (8): The reference remains (8); it is not regularised to (18).
- Scan 038, entire overwritten B definition: The local B correction is not claimed as settled independently of the global cancellation.
- Scan 038, lower display, star on p map: Preserves the written subscript star.
- Scan 038, lower display, second map letter and its star: Preserves j and its subscript star.
- Scan 039, penultimate prose line, final fundamental-object symbol: Preserves the author’s capital fundamental-object notation; no silent replacement by the usual lowercase group notation.
- Scan 040, relation in the first displayed equivalence: The unusual written relation is preserved without replacing it by a more customary equivalence sign.
- Scan 040, final sentence, after n’est pas: Preserves the author’s qualification that the canonical morphism is not necessarily a homotopy equivalence.
- Scan 042, cancelled expression following X: The cancelled claim is retained as ink; it is not used to identify X mathematically.
- Scan 042, X superscript in final list: Apparent authorial notation slip preserved; the list is not silently regularised to X-star.
- Scan 043, display (25), corrected coefficient after S^2: The live coefficient is A; the visible correction from Z is part of the manuscript evidence.
- Scan 043, line 15, first lower Lim; M0964: The transcription preserves an underline rather than silently supplying a colimit arrow.
- Scan 043, line 15, quoted adjective: The topos is described as recollé; H usuels changes the stated gluing context.
- Scan 043, line 16, second lower Lim; M0965: The two lower instances are transcribed as written underlines, without restoring arrows from mathematical expectation.
- Scan 044, Main lines 20–21: half-simplicial term: The manuscript names half-simplicial techniques; H changes the mathematical method to an ordinary prose word.
- Scan 045, two-line heading at top: Restores the section heading and its qualification sans tubes.
- Scan 045, introductory paragraph, final line, first word: Retains the author’s phrase locale trivialité normale des plongements.
- Scan 046, diagram (6), left descending projection; M0970: The left projection is consistently named sigma in the diagram and its explicitly linked property.
- Scan 046, display (7)a, arrow label; M0971: This clear label supplies the page-local notation for the corresponding projection in (6).
- Scan 046, display (7)a, placement and state of sur chaque X_i: The manuscript explicitly qualifies constant by sur chaque X_i.
- Scan 047, line 13, first source/target map name before et b de (6): Keeps the map name consistent with the established source projection of (6).
- Scan 047, display (9), second fibre-product factor, map after X_{Delta_1}: Uses the established source-map name in the composability fibre product.
- Scan 049, line 11, cancelled trial formula after par: Only the cancelled trial relation changes its preferred reading. The live definition below remains an equivalence.
- Scan 050, Raised insertion after the first X_i-prime-star in prose: Restores the explicit identification of each component with X_i-prime-zero times its flag label.
- Scan 050, (17), inverse notation above the initial alpha: Retains the nested written position of the inverse-image exponent.
- Scan 050, (17), bound variable in the set, M0979: The written i^1_* differs from the i_* used in the following condition. Preserve the apparent authorial indexing slip.
- Scan 050, (17), final flag after alpha-star(i-star) equals, M0980: Retains the raised star even though the surrounding flags use a subscript star.
- Scan 050, Later condition, argument of alpha in sans condition: A tentative preferred-reading departure from both witnesses; the intended repeated condition may still be alpha(0)=0. Do not treat omega as a settled new mathematical parameter.
- Scan 053, (23)b, source projection label: Keeps the source projection distinct from the target b; this is notation disambiguation, not a change inferred from the final theorem.
- Scan 053, cancelled les X... after relation d’ordre sur I: This concerns cancelled ink only; it does not alter the live indexing statement.
- Scan 053, parenthetical authorial qualification: The author explicitly qualifies the final claim; the transcription retains this qualification while the notes record a counterexample.
- Scan 055, NB, first X_i after des: Preserve the literal unstarred X_i, even though the subsequent nonempty condition makes the sentence awkward.
- Scan 055, Proof, decoration of the final X_i^*: Keep the written X_i^* in the set difference; do not replace it by the expression the argument might suggest.
- Scan 055, Proof, relation between X_i and X_k: The literal written x in X_i setminus X_k conflicts with the immediately preceding x in X_k; this is preserved as ink, not repaired.
- Scan 055, (28), target of the lower image statement: This is a literal X_0 subscript. The mathematically expected Delta_0 is not substituted.
- Scan 056, attention que les X..., star versus dot: The warning concerns the starred open parts rather than the dotted boundaries.
- Scan 057, (1), raised glyph after right-hand V: The raised ell is part of the written tubular-neighbourhood notation and must not be dropped.
- Scan 057, (2), cancelled upper sign on replacement R: The upper sign is cancelled and therefore is not part of the live definition.
- Scan 057, (3), cancelled upper sign on R_{i,j}: The live notation has no surviving superscript star.
- Scan 057, Left marginal (3bis), continuation X_k subset dot X_j: The written marginal formula asserts a union description of R_ij and its containment in the dotted boundary of X_j.
- Scan 057, (3bis), dot on final X_j: The target is the dotted boundary notation, not all of X_j.
- Scan 057, (4), first R term and its indices: The surviving first term is R_i cap X_i; the old j remains recorded as cancelled.
- Scan 057, Proof, after donc X_k cap X_i subset: The local expression literally ends in bare dotted X. The context suggests an omitted i, but the subscript is not supplied in the transcription.
- Scan 058, (7), overbar on the first right-side term: The first set difference starts from the barred neighbourhood.
- Scan 059, Top formula, between V and N: The top formula has the three-stroke approxeq relation, rather than either witness’s proposed two-stroke relation.
- Scan 059, Top formula, accent over N: The N accent is now explicitly check/tilde, with check preferred; the alternative remains unresolved.
- Scan 059, Between cas and où: H inserts an additional inequality i≤j that is not written in this successor clause.
- Scan 059, Inserted expression after the second Z: The inserted fibre expression is a set difference from Z, rather than the verbal vis-à-vis reading.
- Scan 059, Starred note, first equation: The starred marginal qualification X_i^*=X_i is present; H omits it and the associated restriction.
- Scan 060, (10), first index of R: The written definition removes R_{i,k}; it must not be silently changed to the R_{j,k} that appears in (12).
- Scan 060, First partition-set name: The first defined set name contains a star; the first union label in (12) does not. Both physical forms are retained.
- Scan 061, line 20, second unstarred neighbourhood subscript: The favored two-index reading remains explicit but uncertain; a possible extra written j is not silently discarded.

### Still unresolved

- main line 6, cancelled phrase: \deleted{\unsure{comme des systèmes|comme un système}} — The long stroke crosses the lower phrase and leaves the raised en considérant E_X* comme live. Its middle bodies are merged with the strike; both comme des systèmes and comme un système remain possible. The surviving comma is after the live ces systèmes; a terminal comma in the cancelled phrase is not independently separable from the strike.
- main line 10, cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{munis}} — The cancellation conceals much of the lower word; do not resolve from its replacement considérés.
- marginal line 5, cancelled formula before D-ens.: \deleted{\unsure{\Pi_1(\mathcal V^*_{Y,X})}} — Dense oblique hatching leaves only portions of the roof, parentheses, star and lower indices; the entire formula remains unsure.
- main line 14, M0944: \unsure{la|les} — Terminal mark after the compact vowel may be a separate s or the return of a; grammar after the deletion is not used to decide.
- main line 3, number before Quand: $(\unsure{10|1^\circ})$ — The 1 is an oblique upright; the following small rounded mark is raised. Its position permits an ordinal circle as the blind reader proposed, while the preceding diagram-reference wording supports 10. The ink does not securely distinguish them. The insertion leader ends before groupoïdes; capital Quand is clear.
- marginal line 5, abbreviation: \unsure{ss-gr.} — The small bodies are compressed and crossed by the main text.

### Still unresolved

- line 5 and display (22): \unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F} — Same complex F form at both places; typographical alphabet not established by this page.
- crossed condition after (19): avec $\pi_1(Y)$ réduit à $\{1\}$ — Several cancellation strokes pass through the word centres.
- before est défini: alors — Dense local black crossing.
- est défini: \unsure{défini|définie} — A final dotted i is clear; a following e is not distinct.
- crossed word replaced by {a,b}: somme — Horizontal strike masks most of the word.
- crossed early version below object-set line: \unsure{réunion} \illegible{3} \unsure{des trois} — Long dense stroke coincides with letter bodies; retained provisional grouping.
- crossed interlinear text at right: flèches de sous-catégorie — Horizontal strike and the top of a global diagonal cross this addition.
- first words of large lower deletion: des ens d'objets — Readable ascenders and apostrophe survive, but the strike masks low parts.
- small insertion before de (8): figurer en la [une struck] catégorie — Reduced handwriting plus local strike over small word; no grammatical completion made.
- isolated blackened sign before d'un objet: p — Upper loop is obliterated.
- B definition: B=\Pi_1\deleted{\unsure{\mathcal V_{Y,X}|\mathcal V^*_{Y,X}}}\inserted{\unsure{Y}} — Original V, a star-like mark, a thick fork and the global diagonal overlap. Replacement Y is plausible but not settled. The typographical family mathcal V was supplied by parent; the cancelled layers remain uncertain.
- former argument in C definition: \deleted{\unsure{\mathcal V_{Y,X}|\mathcal V^*_{Y,X}}}\inserted{X^*} — Replacement X-star is clear; original V and indices partly hidden by local hatching. The family is represented by parent-established mathcal V within the retained doubt.
- cancelled sign before lower p map: \varphi — Only lower loop and descender survive cleanly.
- comma in the crossed/inserted condition pair: one surviving comma — One comma is visible, while C duplicates it in sequential transcription.
- inserted implication, exponent on X: \pi_1(X^{\unsure{*|\ell}}) — The raised X mark is thick and has a short returning arm; a compressed star and a looped ell cannot be securely separated. The surrounding notation favors star but does not erase the ambiguity. After the formula there is a distinct dotted i followed by joined s/o, reading iso, and then a closing parenthesis. The double-shaft implication and the single arrow with tilde are clear.
- cancelled lower block, before comme étant: \unsure{définies|définis} — Compressed low ending does not distinctly separate an e.
- lower line Dans le cas: \deleted{\unsure{actuel}} — The local stroke crosses the middle of actuel; the initial a, tall stem and terminal l survive, but not every interior letter can be separated. Retain the word under an unsure mark. Both surviving commas remain outside the local strike; the global cancellation is separate.

### Still unresolved

- three F occurrences: prose lines 6, 7, and final paragraph: \unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F} — Letter F is clear in all three places, but the same handwritten cap/stem/crossbar supports neither printed alphabet decisively.
- line 5 hatted C: \unsure{\widehat C|\widehat{\mathbb C}} — Interior strokes could be intentional doubling or retracing; both witnesses print ordinary C.
- line 8 cancelled C before X=blackboard C: \deleted{\unsure{\mathbb C|C}} — Outer C is readable, but cancellation masks the status of its interior stroke.
- X* formula, cancelled symbol: \deleted{\unsure{\mathbb P}} — The cancellation is dense, with several surviving loops and hooks; no unambiguous letter structure or possible additional indices can be separated.
- final paragraph word topos, underlying strokes: topos — Final topos is readable. Extra strokes are real, but an earlier word cannot be transcribed letter by letter, and the evidence does not justify adding type.
- line 6, infinity-connexe parenthesis: $\infty$-\unsure{connexe|conn} — The independent crop confirms infinity/hyphen but does not expose the full final exe sequence. The original whole-word connu alternative is excluded; the narrower ending doubt remains.
- line 5, cancelled word before (donc: \deleted{\unsure{est|et}} — The cancellation crosses the small middle letter body; the s cannot be isolated securely.
- before displayed equations, cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{donc}} — The lower d and final c survive, but the internal letters merge with the strike.
- penultimate prose line, article before Pi: \unsure{les|le} — The low ending and terminal return do not independently establish a final s.

### Still unresolved

- first displayed label, before F: \deleted{\unsure{(24)|(23)}} — Cancellation obscures the digit contours. The label’s presence and cancellation are certain, its exact digits are not.
- first displayed F, M0952: \unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F} — Handwritten cap, stem and loop do not settle the printed alphabet.
- F after donc, M0953: \unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F} — The simple cap/stem/bar form does not provide an alphabet distinction.
- first V, M0954: \unsure{V|\mathcal V|Z}^*_{z,Z} — Overtracing masks the final letter’s detailed structure. No independent historical glyph is reconstructed.
- contractibility word after the first equals sign: \unsure{contractile|contractiles|contractibles} — The lower stroke of the shorthand has a loop and a small hump beneath the upper bar, allowing barred m as well as a roughly written equals abbreviation. The adjective has only one clear tall post-i stem and no separately secure final s; add singular contractile while retaining the other possibilities.
- third F after le topos, M0955: \unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F} — The repeated hooked cap and crossbar are clear but not diagnostic of font class.
- cancelled words below qui en: \deleted{\unsure{$\overline m$ si les|$=$ si les}} — Two short horizontal remnants precede a low loop and upright like si, then the clear l and returns of les. The old où les does not account for the initial shorthand. Retain the whole deletion as unsure, with barred-m si les / equals si les alternatives. The raised qui en and its attachment are clear.
- opening quote before pièces: \unsure{``} — The opening mark is compact and overlapping; paired closing strokes and the page’s ordinary quotation form support double quotes, but two opening strokes are not cleanly separated.
- opening quote before morceaux: \unsure{``} — The opening has merged dark strokes; the closing pair is distinct. No guillemet angles are visible.
- final F in X-to-topos formula, M0956: \unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F} — The more flourished loop and low foot still admit either printed alphabet.
- first shorthand, before contractibility adjective: $\unsure{\overline m|=}$ — The lower stroke of the shorthand has a loop and a small hump beneath the upper bar, allowing barred m as well as a roughly written equals abbreviation. The adjective has only one clear tall post-i stem and no separately secure final s; add singular contractile while retaining the other possibilities.
- two occurrences before de construction: \unsure{pierres|pièces} — The noun has two angular low shoulders after the dotted i/e rather than a clearly open c, and no independent grave accent is distinguishable. Pierres is favored but pièces remains possible. The first occurrence was separately cropped at [310,1050,1115,1190] and has the same ambiguity. The insertion ends with low n/s-like returns and has no tall terminal t; néanmoins remains preferable to vraiment. Its curved leader ends between pas and le. Capital Pi has a flat roof and distinct legs. The opening quote remains uncertain.
- after de X: $\unsure{=|\overline m}$ — The lower short stroke has a waved onset beneath the upper bar; it may be the equals abbreviation or a compressed barred m. Retain that distinction as a local doubt, without expanding either. The opening quote remains compressed and uncertain.

### Still unresolved

- Main line 8, F: \unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F} — Visible hand does not establish a particular printed alphabet.
- Main line 12, superscript on S: S^{\unsure{2|7}} — The cap and short downward return are 2-like but also permit 7; the retained doubt is now explicit in the page after blind review. P is excluded by the returning lower S stroke.
- Main line 7, after fondée: $\unsure{\overline m|=}$ — The lower stroke is a returning loop followed by a long tail beneath an upper bar, rather than two plainly straight bars. Barred m is favored, with equals shorthand retained as an alternative. The following si is already on the page.
- Main line 9, last word: \unsure{correct|courant} — The initial c/o and final crossed t are distinct, but the low central shoulders can be segmented as rr/ec or ur/an. Correct remains the preferred reading; courant is admitted rather than decided from the sentence alone.
- Main line 12, en ... temps: $\unsure{=|\overline m}$ — The lower short stroke has a returning onset but no distinct full m.

### Still unresolved

- top cancelled anchor and following d-apostrophe: \deleted{\unsure{qu'}}\inserted{que la donnée} d'une — Short strike crosses the q/u region and touches the following d-like stroke; curved upper line is an insertion leader.
- struck word before gerbes: \unsure{recollem.|recolle|recoller} — Horizontal cancellation coincides with low final letter bodies.
- relation in cancelled X-to-S-squared expression: \unsure{\simeq} — Oblique crossings over the old relation obscure its upper contour; S and 2 themselves are readable.
- struck phrase following live d-apostrophe: une ext — Tall t and ext cluster are clearer than the preceding low word; no abbreviation stop is separately visible.
- mark after déduisent: \unsure{,} — Small dark mark has a loop/angular turn rather than a normal single comma curve; not omitted or confidently labelled a numeral.
- first de gerbes: gerbes — The comma and lower body plus accent of à remain. The final short return after gerbe is compressed enough that singular/plural cannot be certified; keep gerbes first, mark gerbe as alternative.
- after cancelled S-squared: \unsure{,} — The cancelled relation must remain symbolic and doubtful, not an illegible-word placeholder. A small dark oblique mark below/right of S-squared could be a comma; its separation from the cancellation is uncertain, so it is marked doubtful.
- quoted noun in last paragraph: pierres — Cross-page ink comparison with scan040, scan044 and the clearer paired r forms of scan058: the compressed middle low turns are compatible with the same pierres word. No independent grave accent is secure. Prefer pierres consistently, while retaining pièces and the blind pavés proposal because this local word is more compressed.

Dans le cas | The prime after X has one hooked stroke, unlike the neighbouring crossed V-star, and Pi_2 has a broad straight cap. The quoted noun has compressed low middle strokes and no clearly separable accent; pièces is plausible but pierres/pavés cannot be excluded by these bodies alone. Final cross-page review: Cross-page ink comparison with scan040, scan044 and the clearer paired r forms of scan058: the compressed middle low turns are compatible with the same pierres word. No independent grave accent is secure. Prefer pierres consistently, while retaining pièces and the blind pavés proposal because this local word is more compressed. ### Still unresolved

- line 4, struck word: \deleted{\unsure{sur}} — The strike largely covers the low letters; opening and terminal curls survive but do not settle all three letters.
- line 5, Hom argument: \unsure{\mathbb Z|Z} — Letter Z is clear, but a thick diagonal cannot securely be distinguished from double-stroke typography. This doubt is local and does not change the clearly multiple-stroked live Z in line 4.
- display (25), old coefficient: \deleted{\unsure{\mathbb Z|Z}}\inserted{A} — The older Z is visible under A, whose strokes obscure the font distinction; replacement by live A is clear.
- line 11, struck phrase: \deleted{\unsure{limites inductives}} — The cancellation crosses the low letter bodies and endings, so the phrase cannot be treated as fully recovered letter by letter.
- line 12, before types: \unsure{des|de} — The short final downward return may encode s or only the end of e.
- line 12, parenthetical connective: \unsure{avec|ou} — Initial dark mark, open cup and extended low return favor the longer compressed reading but do not securely settle every letter.
- after donc, before un élément: \unsure{à|:} — The sign after donc has only a tiny lower body and a detached upper mark. The lower body is too reduced to distinguish accented à from two punctuation points with confidence. Retain à first and colon as an alternative.
- right end of cancelled limites inductives: \unsure{,} — The cancelled words have recognizable tall l/t and inductives strokes but low bodies merge with the cancellation, so their doubt stays. A short dark oblique descends at the right end beyond the final letter returns; a cancelled comma is possible, though its meeting with the strike prevents certainty. Record it as unsure. The inserted capital Lim has a distinct arrowhead below it.
- before la van Kampen: \unsure{à|:} — The first sign is reduced to a short slanted lower stroke and a detached upper mark, comparable to b06. Keep à/colon unresolved; la and underlined van Kampen, including its final comma, are clear.

- paragraph line 4, after relatifs: \unsure{à|:} — The upper mark and compact lower body are visible, but the lower body does not retain enough shape to distinguish a reduced a from a punctuation dot. The clear later à provides a local comparison, not a license to regularize.

- cancelled word below display (1): \deleted{\unsure{parties}} — The strike passes through the old baseline letter bodies. Some descenders and higher strokes remain, but witness agreement cannot establish each buried letter.
- display (2), pair inside the set braces: (x,\unsure{i}) — The dot is clear, but the body is retraced into a small loop and transverse stroke. Context is not used to force an undoubted i.

The point after the last locale meets the dark right edge and is now doubtful. All inequalities, indices, the live insertion and the unmatched opening parenthesis remain.
- (7)b, final point at right scan edge: \unsure{.} — The small mark reaches the black right border and cannot be isolated securely as a full stop.

### Still unresolved

- line 5, apparent cancellation after étant: X étant muni — The a/n bodies are extremely contracted; the cross-stroke is traceable to the final t. Root independently agreed on the crop.
- line 12, M0972: X_{\Delta_1} — The final crossing diagonals establish X; they do not identify a distinct older letter or prove a deletion.
- line 13, source-map name: \sigma — The ink alone leaves the alphabet open. The established source-map notation supplied by the root, together with explicit de (6), decides sigma.
- diagram (9), second fibre-product map: \sigma — Same locally admitted oval form and same source map; the supplied current-run notation applies.
- after objet ordonné: et — The final short word has a low initial curve rising to a tall uncurling stem and a long rightward return, closely resembling the clear et in b12. Prefer et while retaining de as alternative. The awkward resulting wording is not repaired from grammar.
- after catégorie comma: dans — The d loop is clear, but the following low a/n/e bodies are strongly contracted into a nearly level run. Dans remains preferred; des is now explicit rather than ruled out by grammar.

### Still unresolved

- (10), first left-hand X, Delta subscript: \unsure{r} — The stem is filled and retraced; a rightward hook survives, but not the whole clean body seen in later r indices.
- (11), first I argument, delimiter pair: \unsure{\{\Delta_r\}|(\Delta_r)} — Hooked upper parts favor braces, but curved lower portions and retracing leave parentheses possible. Both physical boundaries are reported separately; the whole formula is audited.
- prose after étant: \unsure{des|les} — A lower closed bowl favors d, but the overtraced tall loop does not exclude an l return.
- between (12) and (13), before l’application: \unsure{où|d'où} — The crowded beginning and high mark do not yield a secure letter/apostrophe segmentation. The manuscript does not justify choosing solely from syntax.
- final line, after associé à: une — The low joined word after a has several shallow returns; its final elongated return favors une but does not independently close an e body. Retain une with un now explicit as an alternative, rather than decide from the gender of application.

### Still unresolved

- (16), cancelled glyph after alpha colon: \deleted{\unsure{\alpha}} — The surviving loop and return resemble alpha, but cancellation and contact with the following live Delta obscure a complete letter. The omission in H is certain; the literal recovery remains tentative.
- Later sans condition clause, argument of alpha: \alpha(\unsure{\omega|0})=0 — The local two-bowl open contour favors omega visually. The explicit earlier condition suggests intended zero, but neither that reference nor agreement of C/H closes the open local stroke.
- raised label after drapeaux: i_* — The whole block and existing details r07/r10 were inspected. Composantes has a small low e return before the final s. Before the flag definition the large left curve is separate from the small o and accented u return: retain (ou, not ici/ie. The angular marks above i are short dot forms also seen in neighboring i glyphs, not independent hats. The closed triangular type symbol has a retraced base, not an independent A crossbar. Both cancellations and the raised component identity agree. The inserted label after drapeaux has a closed lower loop and short upper segment; i_* remains preferred from the same flag notation, but the d-like loop now receives an explicit d_* alternative.
- (18), underlined word after imm.: focale — the initial of the final word has an upper loop, a long descender below the underline and a separate horizontal crossing, markedly f-like beside the ordinary l of locales above. Prefer focale with locale explicit as alternative; mathematical expectation does not decide that glyph.

### Still unresolved

- first factor of the Cartesian product in (21): \unsure{I|X} — The first factor is heavily retraced: diagonal outer strokes look X-like, while the thick central stem and serifs support I. The second I is plain. No separate old letter or revision order is asserted; this is a certainty-only qualification.
- remainder of upper cancelled clause: \unsure{pouvoir en déduire} — Several low letters coincide with the strike and with descenders of C'est équivalent. Only a tentative reading is possible.
- tiny cancellation before formelle: \unsure{e} — The crossing strokes leave an e-like body but not a secure complete group.
- point after les before espaces de: \unsure{.} — A definite compact dark point is present, but it interrupts the continuing phrase. It is not silently promoted to secure punctuation.
- words of the old corollary clause after X_Delta1: \unsure{est une partie fermée} — The broad old-line cancellation covers several inner letter bodies. The visible outlines support C's phrase without settling every letter.
- earlier subscript within the inserted family: \deleted{\unsure{\Delta_0|\Delta}} — The delta-like oblique/base survives, but the apparent little 0 at its right merges with dense blackening.
- nested cancelled word after une: \unsure{famille|part} — The blackened word after une contains a tall initial structure and several compressed upright/looped strokes. Famille remains possible, but the shorter part reading cannot be excluded under the hatching, so it is made explicit. The adjacent old predicate has a separate p-like descent; its existing partie fermée doubt remains.
- second broad cancellation at lower left: \unsure{supposons} — Repeated zigzags and descenders overlap the central letters.
- small cancelled fragment below the second supposons: \unsure{où} — The loop, stem, curved return and detached tick permit où but do not settle it. Its placement before the final X_Delta0 follows its physical position, not a reconstructed sentence.
- (21)d, before X_i intersection X_j: on a — The whole formula agrees, including the overwritten first factor I/X, all lower indices and final comma. Between the quantifier and X_i are a short first body, a horizontal low continuation and a separate compact mark. They fit the highly contracted on a seen in this hand, but the letter bodies are not complete; retain on a with explicit doubt rather than treat the marks as an assured dash and dot.
- after formelle: : — The small crossed group retains an e-like loop and remains unsure e, not an undifferentiated illegible character. Both quote pairs are short raised strokes. The two terminal punctuation marks are near the black image edge; the lower blob has a possible small tail, so colon remains preferred with semicolon explicitly possible.
- cancelled noun before drapeaux: simplexes — The old noun has s/i, low m, descending p, tall l and a heavily crossed ending. The x-like crossing favors simplexes, but the strike obscures the e/x versus i/c sequence. Retain simplexes with simplices as a local alternative; the raised drapeaux and its p descender remain separate.
- old corollary before X_Delta1: que — Before the old X_Delta1 a small cancelled loop, descending shaft and low continuation are distinct from the raised et soit, supporting a doubtful que.

### Still unresolved

- (23)b, cancelled word before est: \deleted{\unsure{en}} — The heavy horizontal cancellation covers the second letter; no unqualified resolution of the old C doubt.
- (23)b, old letters over the Xi to Xj shaft: \deleted{\unsure{ds|d}} — The deleted de is clear. Over the thick map shaft a tall d stem and lower loop survive; the remainder is obscured, so preserve ds with d now explicit as alternative. The oval source projection is the same sigma established in scan046, and the following inserted étale belongs to the next line already present in the page.
- cancelled phrase before et and the b map: \deleted{\unsure{et alors|étale}} — The longer surviving tail favors et alors; crossing strokes obscure the beginning. The C struck-text doubt is retained, not resolved.
- b-labelled map X_Delta1 to X_Delta0: X_{\Delta_1}\overset{b}{\unsure{\longrightarrow|-}}X_{\Delta_0} — The right end is a short blunt cap rather than two clear wings. It admits an abbreviated arrow form, but literal dash remains possible. The existing arrow reading is retained with added uncertainty only; no substantive C/H difference is counted.
- cancelled les X... after relation d’ordre: \deleted{\unsure{les $X_j$|les $X_i$}} — Long lower left-returning descender favors j; the strike covers its upper part.
- M0982: X_i\unsure{-|\longrightarrow}X_j — The connector cap does not distinguish a literal dash from a compressed arrowhead. Supplied mark remains undecided.
- lower stroke beneath final capital Lim: \unsure{\underset{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}|\underline{\mathrm{Lim}}} — The right end has an upper point/return but no resolved lower wing. Arrow is retained as primary, with the underline alternative explicit. This added doubt alone is not a correction to C.
- raised word before constant: étale — The inserted word has accented e, a crossed t, a strongly contracted middle connection and tall l/terminal e. Etale remains preferred, but no independent a bowl can be separated, so étle is retained as an explicit alternative. The long curved stroke is its leader, not a parenthesis; constant is followed by comma and a curly brace.
- after imm. locale: . — The long surviving tail in the cancelled phrase favors et alors while its shorter étale alternative stays. The labelled connector has a blunt right cap and remains arrow/dash doubtful. A separate small horizontal dark mark precedes the closing parenthesis after locale; retain it as a doubtful period, with short dash also possible rather than simply omit it.
- cancelled word before avec la partition: et — A broad first curved body and one tall crossed t support et, but the cancellation covers the low middle so that the blind Soit proposal cannot be completely excluded. Keep et preferred with Soit as an explicit cancelled alternative.
- cancelled old Supposons: Supposons — The cancelled baseline has the long initial S, two p descenders and final low o/n/s bodies of Supposons, but the middle is covered by the strike and raised replacement. Keep the word with an explicit doubt, while the new raised Supposons remains clear.
- before l-aise: à — The lower a body is filled and reduced almost to a small wedge, with a separate high stroke. À remains preferred by comparison with the clearer reduced a in b17, but a colon-like reading remains locally possible and is now explicit.

### Still unresolved

- After cela éliminerait comma: $\unsure{\overline{\mathrm{m}}|\overline{\mathrm{vu}}}$ — The overbar is certain; the compressed lower trough/rise/return can be a reduced m or a tightly written vu. A possible expansion même belongs only in the notes and abbreviation list.
- Cancelled phrase after cône dessus: \deleted{\unsure{d'un sommet|d'origine}} — The ends of celles and déduites have low terminal returns; des has its separate final s return, and the raised cercles has a final curved s below its preceding e. Retain the plurals. The shorthand is the barred reduced m/vu form, without expanding it to même. The word cone has no independently secure accent, so prefer the literal unaccented form but retain cône as a doubtful alternative. The old phrase after dessus is largely covered: d-un sommet remains a possible reading; the blind d-origine is now recorded as another possibility, although no clear g descender survives. The crossed n-est pas has a visible apostrophe, p descender and low terminal strokes, but its covered middle warrants an explicit doubt. The live Il matches the same initial form above; its looped l is not changed to Je. Disconnexe has low n/n movements and a crossing x near the end, unlike discontinue; montrera continues with additional low r/a strokes after montre. The four round vertex labels remain consistently 0/o doubtful. The first drawing retains ink visible in the crop: a parallel lower A trace, short stub below a, internal separated strokes and a gap between the left boundary and the ring; the simpler blind rendering omits these.
- d'origine, first prose apex label: \unsure{0|o} — Small oval with closing return; no diagnostic digit-versus-letter stroke.
- locale en, second prose apex label: \unsure{0|o} — Retraced oval with internal strokes; no distinct cancelled precursor can be read, and the live shape does not settle zero versus o.
- dont parentheses, third prose apex label: \unsure{0|o} — Small open-join oval compatible with both forms; keep the repeated label consistent without asserting a font distinction.
- Below the apex of the marginal drawing: \unsure{0|o} — Retraced oval matching the same point label in prose, without a decisive zero-specific feature.
- before dessus: cone — The ends of celles and déduites have low terminal returns; des has its separate final s return, and the raised cercles has a final curved s below its preceding e. ### Still unresolved

- NB short-form span after le: \unsure{sous. ens|sous-ens. ord.} — Two short groups are visible. A separate third ord. group and its d are not securely located.
- NB word before the family: \unsure{clairem\textsuperscript{t}|clairement} — The terminal upper hook can be read as raised t or as part of the full ending; preferred short form is tentative.
- NB short cancelled fragment: \deleted{\unsure{sa|ca}} — Only two low rounded bodies survive the cross-stroke. The possible connection with satisfont is interpretive and is not supplied in the text.
- NB cancelled d'ailleurs: \deleted{\unsure{d'ailleurs}} — The cancellation hides several central letters.
- NB raised replacement: \inserted{\unsure{satisfont}} — Several letters are crossed by or merge with neighboring layer strokes.
- NB lower cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{satisfont}} — The independent horizontal cancellation obscures the word's middle.
- Proof old word after donc: \deleted{\unsure{alors}} — Scope of the strike is clear, but the middle letters are not all independently visible; H's certain form does not settle them.
- Proof parenthesized cancellation: \deleted{(\unsure{car})} — The small crossed bodies admit car without exposing every letter.
- Proof cancelled interlinear formula: \deleted{$\unsure{x\in X_i\cap X_j=}$} — The enlarged cancelled formula has a small initial x, the curved membership mark, two large crossed X bodies, a low i, a cap-like middle curve, a long j descender and two terminal equality strokes. This supports x in Xi cap Xj =, as the blind reader proposed, rather than k<=i ou k<=j. The equality bars are visible beyond the main strike. Because the membership/cap joins are partly crossed, the recovered whole expression remains explicitly doubtful.
- Cancelled word before plus généralement: \deleted{\unsure{par}} — A p-like start and low ending favor par, but the crossing strokes prevent a secure reading.
- (28), right fermé-gloss subscript: X_{\unsure{\Delta_r|\Delta_0}} — The final small index reaches the right source edge and cannot be discriminated securely.
- proof, ending of disjoint after mutuellement: \unsure{disjoint|disjoints} — The word mutuellement is divided across lines without a written hyphen; joining its parts is unchanged. After the tall t of disjoint, the low exit stroke is not a separately formed s, so prefer singular disjoint while retaining disjoints as a doubtful alternative. No sont occurs. The live est remains. Par has a continuous low return after its descending p, without a separately raised r that would establish a written abbreviation.
- (27), immediately after first equals of second row: \unsure{:} — The complete two-row brace and formulas were inspected. Just after the first equals on the second row there are two tiny detached dark points, slightly diagonal: preserve a doubtful colon after that equals, in its actual order =:. They are too small to certify as intentional punctuation, so the colon stays unsure. The final comma is independent.

### Still unresolved

- first alpha argument: \alpha(\unsure{0|\omega})=0 — Opening-parenthesis curve and following small loop are joined and resemble omega. The merger permits (0), whereas the right-hand zero is a separate plain circle; mathematical expectation does not settle certainty.
- M1001: \unsure{-|\longrightarrow} — Only a short downward terminal cap is resolved.
- cancelled phrase before NB: \deleted{\unsure{Dans le cas}} — Dans and the last oval/return group support the C phrase, but le and the c start are crossed.
- cancelled formula after X1=X: \deleted{$\unsure{Y\subset X}$} — The hatching crosses letters and covers the relation. Whole formula retained in doubt, with no illegibility placeholder replacing its symbols.
- cancelled word after old formula: \deleted{\unsure{fermé}} — Tall f-like stroke and low tail survive, but every letter is not independently readable.
- Y subscript of final scripted V: \mathcal{V}_{\unsure{Y},X} — Forked upper part and descending stem support Y; lower looping retracing prevents certainty. No independent cancellation is established.
- quoted non singulière, ending: singulière — The final part of singulière is tightly reduced: after the tall l and dotted i there is a low two-bend return, without a fully separable last e body. Singulière remains preferred, with the blind singulier now explicit as an alternative. Both raised quote pairs, following de, unstarred object and separate dotted object are clear.

### Still unresolved

- heading, aux: aux — The low ending is compatible with a cursive x but not fully separated from the u return. C preferred word unchanged; the draft adds a local uncertainty.
- citation after (1), closer: \unsure{)} — The tall curved closer carries an extra cross/loop and a small upper dot. The § and numeral 1 are clear; no second symbol is invented.
- (2), cancelled first V trial: \unsure{\mathcal V_{ij}|\overline{\mathcal V}_{ij}} — V/i/j survive, but no distinct overbar can be separated from the hatching. Bare V is the tentative first reading. This remains an unresolved local decoration doubt.
- (2), cancelled old R_j: \unsure{R_j} — R bow/leg and a j-like descending hook remain under heavy crossing; exact old form is not fully exposed.
- (2), upper cancelled sign on replacement R: \unsure{*} — Multiple crossing strokes obscure the original sign. No live star is asserted.
- (3), upper cancelled sign on R: \unsure{*} — A second independently cancelled upper sign has surviving loop/stem fragments but no diagnostic star arms.
- (3), crossed first X_k under union: \unsure{X_k} — The X and k fragments are plausible but the whole old term is hatched; a possible following relation cannot be separated and is not supplied.
- abandoned crossed trial below (3), union: \unsure{\bigcup} — The large U-shaped bottom and right upright support union, but crossing strokes mask the complete original sign.
- same abandoned trial, short phrase: \unsure{\text{tel q}} — The t/e/l-like sequence and long q descender are visible but crossed. No expansion to tel que or tels que is made.
- crossed proof phrase, et: \unsure{\text{et}} — The long wavy strike crosses the space between X_i and ell; two proposed letters are not independently clear.
- small replacement symbol in (2): R — The narrow loop with lower right foot beside the right-hand script V matches the looped ell forms in the proof, so retain ell. The section reference has an upright stem and lower foot of 1; its peculiar following closer remains separately doubtful. The hatching over the first trial still leaves the script V bowl and descending j, with no independently secure bar or star. The crossed large R_j stays doubtful. The small replacement has a closed low loop, short upright left segment and heavy right descending return: R remains preferred, but an a-like reading is now admitted explicitly; its i, comma, j and replacement position are clear. The upper cancelled sign remains an unsure star, not a new live decoration.

### Still unresolved

- (7), old union-index glyph: \deleted{\unsure{i|\ell}} — The surviving lower loop and long curved blackened upper body admit ell as well as i. Retain i as the first proposal and add ell. The short wavy underline is directly under k, not under the less-than sign. The separate old horizontal mark is crossed by short upward teeth; an unsure dash remains a more informative cautious reading than an unidentified character.
- (7), old mark above second-row overbar: \deleted{\unsure{-}} — Long horizontal ink with teeth of cancellation establishes the old mark but not its original function.
- diagram (9), M1005: \unsure{\mathcal{V}_{ij}^*|X_{ij}^*} — Full bowl/cap and crossing diagonals coexist; no readable separate old glyph can be assigned.
- last line, M1006: \unsure{comprenant|comprennent} — The a/e body in the ending is reduced to a flat low stroke.

### Still unresolved

- Adjective after trop: \unsure{généraux|généreux} — The reduced post-r vowel does not discriminate a from e. Générales lacks a supporting tall l.
- Deleted word before homéomorphe: \deleted{\unsure{est}} — The cancellation merges the middle letter strokes; H's certain est is not used to remove C's honest doubt.
- Starred note, punctuation after second Xi: \unsure{,} — The old crossed group below this position prevents secure isolation of a comma. C's proposed mark is retained only as doubtful; root independently found it unresolved.
- Starred note, old words: \deleted{\unsure{des cas}} — Dense overstrikes conceal the individual letter bodies.
- Large drawing, parenthesized left index: \unsure{\overline{\mathcal V}_{ij}} — The overbar and main letter are visible, while the dark retraced subscript remains unresolved.
- Top formula, first neighbourhood symbol: \unsure{\mathcal V|\dot{\mathcal V}} — The top relation has two waves plus a straight lower bar, equivalent to the blind underlined-approx encoding. The scripted V has an attached cap, not a detached overbar. A small dark point lies inside its upper bowl; its function is not secure, and the blind dotted-V proposal is now admitted as an alternative to the preferred unaccented letter. The small N accent has an angular valley and short hooked entry; check remains preferred, but a tilde interpretation cannot be excluded. All d indices, stars, prime counts, the lower zero subscripts and the page84 reference agree.
- Top formula, accent on N: \unsure{\check N|\widetilde N} — The top relation has two waves plus a straight lower bar, equivalent to the blind underlined-approx encoding. The scripted V has an attached cap, not a detached overbar. A small dark point lies inside its upper bowl; its function is not secure, and the blind dotted-V proposal is now admitted as an alternative to the preferred unaccented letter. The small N accent has an angular valley and short hooked entry; check remains preferred, but a tilde interpretation cannot be excluded. All d indices, stars, prime counts, the lower zero subscripts and the page84 reference agree.
- Main prose, initial case of standart: \unsure{standart|Standart} — The main phrase has du: the d is followed by an open u return, unlike the more looped e in the sideways de type. Normale ends at e before the parenthesis. Both quote pairs are straight marks. The initial letter of standart is unusually tall and angular; lower case remains preferred, but the blind capital-S alternative is added. The final crossed t is retained literally.
- After first homéomorphe callout: \unsure{à|:} — The cancelled est has a looped e and tall final t, but the middle strokes merge with cancellation; retain its doubt. The following callout is a raised star. The mark before un fibré is a tiny lower rounded/wedge body and detached upper stroke: reduced à is preferred, but the blind colon is now admitted. The script V, i,j comma and star are clear.
- Before qui est une variété topologique: \unsure{(} — The opening curved stroke is crowded against the q of qui, and a separate opening parenthesis cannot be isolated confidently; mark that opener doubtful while retaining the clear closing parenthesis. The two tiny marks after that closer resemble the reduced à elsewhere, but also permit a colon; add the explicit alternative. There is no separate comma at this point.
- After first topologique parenthesis: \unsure{à|:} — The opening curved stroke is crowded against the q of qui, and a separate opening parenthesis cannot be isolated confidently; mark that opener doubtful while retaining the clear closing parenthesis. The two tiny marks after that closer resemble the reduced à elsewhere, but also permit a colon; add the explicit alternative. There is no separate comma at this point.

The inserted parenthesis has a two-stroke simeq, first Z with ij and a star, second Z with i,j, set difference and an angular small z in braces; the crossing f belongs to fibre above and does not change z to 2. In the following C subscript, the minute point/short body before the long j favors ij, but a lone dotted j is possible; explicitly retain Cij/Cj. The insertion leader and its attachment after R times C remain.
- NB before un fibré en cônes: \unsure{à|:} — Top and compacte are separately underlined on separate wrapped lines, within the already underlined phrase. A separate curved opener lies just before NB, distinct from the N body; retain it. The reduced two-mark à before un fibré has the same colon-like ambiguity as b09/b10 and is now qualified. The underline beneath cônes, Cij, ellipsis, closing parenthesis, period and capital Quand remain visible.
- Sideways note, initial case of standart: \unsure{standart|Standart} — The repeated sideways phrase has de with a compressed e, then type and the literal final crossed t of standart. The tall initial permits either lower-case s or capital S; record the same explicit alternative as in the full margin box b13. This is one physical word, not an extra counted correction.

### Still unresolved

- opening cancelled neighbourhood sign: \deleted{$\unsure{\mathcal V_{ij}}$} — The bowl/hook and small index strokes survive only partly. Cancellation does not justify removing the original doubt.
- (10), old first R index: \deleted{\unsure{j}}\inserted{i} — The lower descending hook is crossed. The separate upper dotted i remains plain and is not conflated with the descending j of (12).
- (10), final union term: \unsure{S_{j,k}|\Sigma_{j,k}} — Curved and angular layers coexist in the retracing. The following S definitions do not authorize removing a genuine local ink doubt.
- (10), trailing short mark: \unsure{\deleted{m}|\underline{m}} — The low horizontal stroke can be cancellation or baseline underlining; internal hatching/retracing is also ambiguous. The whole mark, not merely its state, is doubtful.
- inline definition of I^(k), crossed word: \deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{def}|\text{déf}}} — Crossing bars obscure whether an independent accent was present. Cancellation is clear even though the accent is not.
- inline definition of I^(k), comparison: \unsure{\ell<k|\ell\leq k} — The thick comparator merges with the ell foot. < is favored but is not resolved from the later desired boundary formula.
- (12), lower union, prime count: \unsure{I^{(k)''}|I^{(k)'}} — The final tiny joined strokes favor a double prime but do not expose two as independently as the earlier explicit second-set label. Both witnesses' preferred double prime remains first.
- cancelled neighbourhood sign before On a donc: \deleted{$\unsure{\mathcal V_{ij}}$} — The bowl, hook, dot and descending index are visible through crossing strokes, but not every original stroke can be isolated.
- bottom paragraph, cancelled word after être: \deleted{\unsure{un}} — The thick horizontal cancellation masks most of the low letter bodies; two humps are compatible with un but are not fully plain.
- Opening, before first closer: \unsure{topos|topo} — The opening word plutôt has the appropriate tall stems and accent, while the dash is a single written horizontal. The final low body of topos is compressed against the closer; the blind topo cannot be excluded, so qualify the final s. In the cancelled neighbourhood sign, the round bowl and upper cap support the existing unsure script V; the crossing diagonal belongs to the cancellation and does not establish a separately recoverable X. The second-line closing curve and following comma remain, without an added exclamation.
- NB abbreviation after k: \unsure{succ.|success.} — The full note has an opening parenthesis, one NB underline, the long-stemmed phi form and no stop after j=k. After k, the compressed short word and point favor succ.; additional reduced s/e-like curves also permit the independent success. reading, so retain that as an explicit abbreviation alternative. There is no full esseur ending or est un. The final closing curve remains separate.
- First partition containment relation: \unsure{\not\supset|\not\supseteq} — The first label has a multi-arm star and one prime, while the second has two separate upper prime ticks despite retracing of its (k). Preserve those literal labels. The lower stroke of the first crossed containment sign is darkly doubled; the preferred not-superset remains, but a fused additional equality stroke cannot be excluded, so admit not-superseteq explicitly. The second containment sign has no crossing and remains unchanged.
- Aussi intersection, second index of R: \unsure{k|n} — The first R index is a live descending j, not the revised i of formula10. The lower union prime count remains doubtful. The large oblique comma follows the lower summand and is not supplied after the brace. In the lower aussi formula, the R second index is unusually low and hump-shaped: k remains preferred by the same-page contracted k forms, but n is physically possible and is now explicit. Preserve the literal dotted X_i, not the expected X_j, and no final stop.
- Final paragraph after germes comma: \unsure{o} — The cancelled un remains tentative. All three singular homéomorphe/homotope endings have no independent s. The final de after affaibli has a long e exit, without a separate s. After germes and its comma is a distinct small dark loop with a short top stroke, before the separately begun qu-il; an enlarged view of this part of the existing blind crop confirms the independent o-like proposal. Add unsure o without assigning a meaning or a cancellation state. On voit has a low v/o, detached raised i and final t, not sait or est. Pouvant, the continuous g-descender of singulière, its period, the unmatched closing parenthesis and final formula comma remain.

### Still unresolved

- line 10, cancelled phrase: \deleted{\unsure{ce sont}} — Crossing and retracing interrupt the low letter bodies, although the overall ce sont length and visible remains fit.
- line 12, verb ending: \unsure{s'exprimera|s'exprime} — Final e/r/a boundaries cannot be separated from the flattened humps and long return; the same-page d’exprimer comparison does not settle the ending.
- line 14, first fibre subscript: \unsure{z|Z} — Small zigzag favors z, but its attached high curved stroke leaves the upper extent unexplained. The whole formula is included in the audit.
- line 20, second unstarred neighbourhood subscript: \unsure{i,j|ijj} — The middle undotted hook is favored as a large comma, but has a J-like left foot; final j is separate.
- line 20, after i≤j: \unsure{sans|dans} — The insertion les Xi star et is above the baseline and its curved leader attaches after the compressed aussi. The first lower index has a long undotted middle hook; its unusually J-shaped comma versus extra j remains unresolved, as both readers allow. The initial stroke of the last word after i<=j has a tall angular entry and flattened continuation: sans remains preferred, but the blind dans is physically possible, so add it explicitly as an alternative. The lower neighbouring seul- belongs to seulement on the following line, not a new word here.
- line 22, before égard: \unsure{en|eu} — The second letter has a flattened body compatible with n or u; the rising entry favors n only provisionally.
- line 23, after diviseurs: \unsure{à|:} — The mark between diviseurs and croisements consists of a tiny lower wedge and a short raised accent-like stroke. The reduced à comparison is persuasive but the blind colon remains possible at this scale. Retain à as primary and explicitly add colon as the alternative. The final crossed x of normaux and closing parenthesis remain clear; voir is neighbouring context outside the narrower blind selection.
